# first-client Plan

The first installation: a hardware shop (quincaillerie), on its own computer (Windows, Docker Desktop or WSL), free
of charge, released once everything below is built and certified (2026-10-20 00:49). It runs in production from then
on and updates itself at night, so nothing done after it may break what exists or lose data.

## Decisions Log
- [2026-10-17 16:31] AGREED: The release goal comes first: the tool-audit queue (steps 3b, 3c, 4, 5) and the i18n goal's order are paused, not dropped, and resume after the release.
- [2026-10-17 16:31] AGREED: Must-have beside invoices (recurring), products (stock and price lists), expenses (recurring), delivery notes, « À surveiller », reports, subscriptions and the demo: the phone as scanner, quotes, the import of their data (their Excel file: the file is adapted or the importer is, whichever keeps them from retyping), and a counter sale.
- [2026-10-17 16:31] AGREED: The installation supports Windows 10/11, Linux and macOS; the shop's machine is most likely Windows 10/11. Reached on localhost and over HTTPS on the LAN address, for the phones.
- [2026-10-17 16:31] AGREED: The app is in production from the first install: an update never breaks what exists or loses data, and installs as easily on premises as hosted.
- [2026-10-17 16:31] AGREED: A demo sign-in (demo@example.com) can try everything; everything it makes is stamped as demo, and all of it is deleted when it signs out: a demo only.
- [2026-10-17 16:31] AGREED: Internationalisation, Arabic and localisation are wanted in the Wednesday version.
- [2026-10-17 16:41] AGREED: Wednesday adds supplier orders and reports to the built modules and the release work.
- [2026-10-17 16:41] AGREED: Arabic ships in full on Wednesday, printed documents included; the developer, a native Arabic speaker, reviews the wording.
- [2026-10-17 16:41] AGREED: The demo gives each sign-in its own private copy of the demo company: every PDF stamped « DÉMO — sans valeur fiscale », mail captured and never sent, nothing reaching the shop's data, deleted at sign-out or after 2 h idle.
- [2026-10-17 16:41] AGREED: The client's Excel file arrives tonight or tomorrow, as a copy with made-up values and as the real file, under var/claude/client-excel/ (gitignored, never committed or sent anywhere).
- [2026-10-17 16:41] AGREED: No compromise: every rule, maturity round and quality bar holds; anything found that breaks a rule or is a mess that works is corrected properly, proven by tests.
- [2026-10-17 16:43] AGREED: Whatever is not 100% on Wednesday 10:00 Tunisia time (09:00 UTC) is switched off, or hidden for a language, and arrives in the first update through the safe update path; nothing half-done is visible to the shop.
- [2026-10-17 16:43] AGREED: The run's order: 1 release core (install on Windows, Linux and macOS, safe updates, security, LAN HTTPS), 2 the demo sandbox, 3 the language foundations and RTL, 4 the proof pass of every built module in fr, en and ar with the client's Excel file, 5 reports, 6 supplier orders, 7 the Arabic texts and documents, 8 guides, help and README on the final screens.
- [2026-10-17 16:43] AGREED: The developer reviews the Arabic wording in the running app and in the source, no marking page; Arabic is shown to the shop only once reviewed.
- [2026-10-17 16:52] AGREED: The shop's install answers on https://localhost on its own PC (passkeys, password and code) and on its LAN address over a local certificate authority the phones trust once (pairing; password and code), detected at every start; a real name for the LAN address may come later, at the price of passkeys enrolled again.
- [2026-10-17 16:52] AGREED: Backups on premises go continuously, encrypted, to a local repository on a second disk or USB drive, and to an off-site bucket the developer provides, which can be switched on and off; the copy taken before every update is always local.
- [2026-10-17 16:53] AGREED: The Windows PC runs Docker Desktop, installed by the shop under Docker's own terms (free under 250 employees and 10 M$ revenue; their current text to be read before the install); we distribute none of it.
- [2026-10-17 16:53] AGREED: Updates pull public images from GHCR by digest: the developer sets PUBLISH_IMAGES and makes the three packages public; the shop's PC builds nothing.
- [2026-10-17 16:59] AGREED: On premises the monitor is optional: an install with its values empty still starts; a failed backup or update, or a rollback, reaches the operator's notifications and goes to the developer by mail; the monitor joins later through two values, without a release.
- [2026-10-17 17:03] AGREED: Updates follow a policy held in the install's values and changed by one command: auto (applied only inside a daily window set from the shop's real hours, a missed window waiting for the next day, never at start), notify (the owner is told in the app; someone runs « Mettre à jour »), or off; changing it from an app screen comes later.
- [2026-10-17 17:03] AGREED: The installer neither checks nor requires disk encryption for now; an autonomous protection of the data at rest that asks nothing of the client is to be researched after Wednesday.
- [2026-10-17 17:03] AGREED: On premises mail goes through the shop's own Gmail account with an app password (smtp.gmail.com:587), required as today: invitations, resets, documents to customers and alerts to the developer leave from the shop's address.
- [2026-10-17 17:08] AGREED: An update's automatic rollback restarts the previous release's images on the current schema (all migrations of an update in one transaction, only additive ones allowed); when those are unhealthy too, nothing is wiped: the developer gets a mail and an in-app alert with the exact restore commands and the pre-update backup's label.
- [2026-10-17 17:08] AGREED: The developer sets PUBLISH_IMAGES now and makes the three GHCR packages public once the first publish creates them; the developer runs the Windows installer on a Windows 10/11 PC on Monday or Tuesday and sends its output.
- [2026-10-17 17:08] ASSUMED (review): The database archives to the local repository only and restic copies that encrypted folder to the bucket when it is on, since a second pgBackRest repository that cannot be reached holds WAL in pg_wal until it answers (pgBackRest user guide, multiple repositories); the update window is read in the shop's zone, Africa/Tunis, while the backup schedule stays UTC. Alternatives: repo2 S3 in pgBackRest.
- [2026-10-17 17:11] ASSUMED (review): The backup layer is split into what archives (compose.backup.yaml) and where to: compose.backup-bucket.yaml (a server's bucket, every value required as today) and compose.backup-local.yaml (the shop's folder, a Docker volume or a host path in BACKUP_DIRECTORY), with compose.backup-offsite.yaml as the bucket switch, which copies the local repositories off site with restic. Alternatives: a local SeaweedFS bucket on the shop PC, which leaves the server files untouched but adds a running service only to satisfy a layer that insists on S3.
- [2026-10-17 17:41] ASSUMED (review): The shop's script (scripts/onprem/twes) asks deploy.sh --files <values> for the installation's compose files, so the TWES_INSTALL/BACKUP_OFFSITE choice lives in one place; the rollback target is the last 'deployed' or 'started' line of releases/history, the pre-update backup's label kept beside it under state/. Alternatives: pinning COMPOSE_PROJECT_NAME and reaching containers by name; a second copy of the layer list.
- [2026-10-17 17:41] ASSUMED (review): Alerts on premises leave twice: mailed from the host with curl over SMTP (STARTTLS, the shop's own account from MAILER_DSN) to ALERT_EMAIL, the developer's address in the install values, so a mail leaves while the app is down; and recorded in the app by app:installation:tell in a new Installation context, queued under state/ while the API does not answer and delivered at the next start or tick. Failures, rollbacks and an unhealthy deep check go to the operators (Platform); an update available under 'notify' goes to each company's owners (Company). Alternatives: mail through the app's own operator notifications, which fail exactly when the app is down.
- [2026-10-17 17:41] ASSUMED (review): A new install's policy is notify (the developer runs the first updates by hand), the window blank until 'twes policy auto HH:MM-HH:MM'; with no network at start LAN_HOST falls back to 127.0.0.1, so the shop's own computer still opens https://localhost; the checkout lives inside WSL's own filesystem, and .gitattributes holds every text file to LF so no checkout reads dirty. Alternatives: auto by default; refusing to start without a network.
- [2026-10-17 19:34] ASSUMED (review): A shop updates only to a release tag signed by the developer's GPG key (0C537690706EB7F5): tag-release.sh signs (git tag -s), the installer pins the public key in a keyring of the installation's own under ~/.twes-in/, and twes update refuses, mails and tells the operators of a tag that keyring cannot verify — because a GitHub account or origin compromise would otherwise ship images to every shop. Alternatives: HTTPS and digests only (a forged tag names forged digests); an SSH allowed-signers file (the developer signs with GPG already).
- [2026-10-17 19:34] ASSUMED (review): The Windows scheduled tasks run wsl.exe plainly by default; install.ps1 -HideWindows tries conhost.exe --headless for the quarter-hourly tick, to be judged on the developer's Monday run — because VBScript wrappers are deprecated and the headless switch is undocumented. Alternatives: S4U tasks (WSL in a non-interactive session unverified), a long-running loop window.
- [2026-10-17 19:34] ASSUMED (review): Order of step 1d: Windows (install.ps1 and twes install) first and pushed for Monday, then Linux systemd, then macOS launchd marked untried; macOS ships in an update if it is not ready by Wednesday — because the client runs Windows. Alternatives: all three together.
- [2026-10-17 20:58] ASSUMED (review): A release tag the pinned key does not verify is alerted to the operators once from tick under every policy, notify included, and on each manual twes update — because a forged tag is a security alarm, not update news. Alternatives: alert only when an update is attempted (silent under the default policy).
- [2026-10-17 20:58] ASSUMED (review): install.ps1 carries the release key's fingerprint and verifies the checked-out tag against it before anything from the checkout runs; the shop gets install.ps1 from the developer out of band — because twes install shows the checkout's own key, which a forged tag controls. Alternatives: rely on the typed yes alone; a GitHub tag ruleset alone (recommended in addition, the developer's action).
- [2026-10-17 20:58] ASSUMED (review): Linux schedules start and tick as systemd system units (User= the account's UID, Requires/After docker.service, a quarter-hour timer) written by twes schedule through sudo, and twes install runs it on Linux — because Docker Engine is a system service there. Alternatives: user units with lingering; cron.
- [2026-10-17 21:59] AGREED: Demo sandboxes are built ahead by the worker through the real use cases (a pool of two ready ones, each with its own throwaway owner); a sign-in takes one, and the worker builds its replacement.
- [2026-10-17 21:59] AGREED: The demo is entered by an « Essayer la démo » button on the sign-in page, no password; the account shows as demo@example.com.
- [2026-10-17 21:59] AGREED: Mail a sandbox would send is captured, never sent, and listed on a « Courriers de la démo » page inside it; it goes with the sandbox.
- [2026-10-17 21:59] AGREED: The demo is on by default, a shop's own install included; the platform operator can switch it off.
- [2026-10-17 22:22] AGREED: A visitor picks the demo's country on « Essayer la démo »: Tunisia (Carthage Conseil) or France (Atelier Mercier); the pool keeps one ready sandbox of each.
- [2026-10-17 22:22] AGREED: A sandbox's audit journal lines stay when it is deleted (field names only, about 66 kB a demo, under a company id that no longer exists); everything else goes. Amends the earlier ruling that all of it is deleted: the journal's protection from the app's own database account is unchanged.
- [2026-10-17 22:22] AGREED: Factur-X (the XML and factur-x.pdf) is refused in a sandbox with « Non disponible dans la démo »; the stamped plain PDF still downloads.
- [2026-10-17 22:22] AGREED: A demo PDF carries « DÉMO — sans valeur fiscale » in a fixed band across every page, top and bottom; the diagonal watermark keeps showing the document's state (BROUILLON, ANNULÉE, PAYÉ).
- [2026-10-17 22:39] ASSUMED (review): A demo owner counts as having proved itself (step-up) at all times — because a sandbox owns nothing real and its account has no password or passkey, so erasure, imports, exports, leaving the customer screen and closing would be dead ends; checked with advisor(). Alternatives: an empty-password proof; refusing those actions in a demo.
- [2026-10-17 22:39] ASSUMED (review): The accountant's export files (accounting.export) are refused in a sandbox with Factur-X — because the Factur-X ruling's reason, no machine-readable invoice ever leaves a demo, names the same class. Alternatives: refuse Factur-X alone; plain CSV list exports stay allowed.
- [2026-10-17 22:39] ASSUMED (review): A sandbox company is named after its catalogue company plus « · démo » and six characters of its id (« Carthage Conseil · démo 7F3A0C ») — because company names are unique (CompanyNameTaken) and the development fixture and e2e find the demo companies by their names. Alternatives: let sandboxes share the name and exclude them from every lookup by name.
- [2026-10-17 22:39] ASSUMED (review): A sandbox owner's stored address is demo-<sandbox id>@example.com and the account shows demo@example.com — because an address is unique per account and the ruling names what the visitor sees. Alternatives: show the stored address.
- [2026-10-17 22:39] ASSUMED (review): A ready sandbox is never rebuilt for freshness; a build stopped halfway (state building for over an hour) is removed with its company and owner so its country is queued again — because each build leaves about 440 journal lines for good, and a stuck build would keep its country's demo closed. Alternatives: rebuild ready sandboxes daily.
- [2026-10-17 23:19] ASSUMED (review): Demo S3: a demo visitor is refused (403 demo_refused) what would make the throwaway account a real one or reach past the sandbox: deleting the account, changing the password, every two-factor and passkey enrolment, creating a company, inviting a member, granting support access, closing the company down, declaring a subscription payment and asking to be told of a planned module — because each either needs a real identity or reaches the operators or the visitor's real mailbox. Alternatives: hide them on screen only (refused: the API is the boundary).
- [2026-10-17 23:19] ASSUMED (review): Demo S3: GET /api/auth/demo answers whether the demo is open and its countries with the catalogue company's name, so the sign-in page shows the button only when a visitor can use it. Alternatives: always show it and answer demo_off on click.
- [2026-10-17 23:19] ASSUMED (review): Demo S3: the members list shows a demo owner as demo@example.com like Me does, so the stored throwaway address never shows anywhere. Alternatives: leave the members list as stored.
- [2026-10-17 23:19] ASSUMED (review): Demo S3: with no ready sandbox the entry answers 503 demo_busy with Retry-After 30 s and tops the pool up, the screen saying « La démo se prépare : réessayez dans un instant. » — a build takes seconds. Alternatives: build one in the request (refused: minutes of dataset in a request).
- [2026-10-17 23:19] ASSUMED (review): Demo S3: entries limited to 10 an hour per client address (demo_entry limiter), so a script cannot drain the pool. Alternatives: no limit; a lower one.
- [2026-10-17 23:19] ASSUMED (review): Demo S3: « Mon compte » hides the two-factor, password and account-deletion sections in a demo session, since the API refuses them; the toast « Non disponible dans la démo » covers anything still offered elsewhere. Alternatives: show them disabled.
- [2026-10-17 23:19] ASSUMED (review): Demo S3: a claim is written to the sandbox company's journal as demo.sandbox_claimed under its owner, the visitor's first audit line. Alternatives: not audited.
- [2026-10-17 23:38] AGREED: Windows install offers two routes, picked at the shop: A, install.ps1 with Docker Desktop as today; B, Docker Engine inside WSL Ubuntu with no Docker Desktop, systemd running start and tick, Windows waking WSL, phones through WSL mirrored networking. Both keep the containers and everything up whatever happens: a restart of the computer, of Docker, or any other event.
- [2026-10-17 23:38] AGREED: Under make up, a migration added while the live stack runs is applied by itself (the one-shot migrate service, as owner, then the grants), so live mode never serves code ahead of its database. Development only; production migrates at deploy as before.
- [2026-10-18 00:05] AGREED: Demo scope of the first release: off by default on a shop's own computer (twes install writes demo.enabled=false), so the shop never sees « Essayer la démo »; S4–S8 (PDF band, Factur-X refusal, removal at sign-out/2 h, hidden from the operator's lists) are built right after route B, and a later release turns the demo on everywhere as ruled.
- [2026-10-18 00:05] AGREED: Supersedes 00:05 (the developer changed the answer): the demo stays on by default everywhere, a shop's own computer included, so anyone can explore without consequences; therefore S4–S8 (PDF band, Factur-X refusal, removal at sign-out/2 h, hidden from the operator's lists and DataHealth, operator switch, e2e) are finished before the release tag, after route B.
- [2026-10-18 00:11] AGREED: Native apps: one front end. No Flutter (a second front end splits one canon per screen and weakens RGAA on the web). After the release the Angular app becomes installable (PWA); Capacitor for the stores or Tauri for shop hardware only on a measured need; Flutter reopened only if neither can do the job. To be carried into SPEC § 7 with the next docs change.
- [2026-10-18 00:53] AGREED: Document links before the tag: the BL page shows its invoice(s) and the invoice its BL numbers with links (read from InvoiceLine.sourceDeliveryNoteLineId), a quote's lines onto an open invoice (row 259), and InvoiceDeliveryNotes reaching Invoices through a port, with ModuleBoundariesTest no longer skipping Domain. After the first release: devis -> BL with per-line delivered/owed tracking on the quote and the commande client question.
- [2026-10-18 01:24] ASSUMED (review): S5 captures a demo mail by its recipient being a sandbox owner's address (any recipient matching keeps the whole mail, rejected before the transport), not by the company a mail concerns: every mail path reaches the mailer with an address, few carry a company. DemoMailReachTest lists every Symfony mailer writer with its reason so a new sending path is classified. Alternatives: a company-aware Mailer port (every caller changed), a separate demo transport per sandbox (no per-sandbox routing in Symfony Mailer).
- [2026-10-18 02:58] AGREED: A demo session alone gets a 2 h idle limit (real accounts keep 30 min); its sandbox is removed when that session ends, at sign-out or after 2 h without a request, so a visitor can step away up to 2 h and find everything as left.
- [2026-10-18 03:06] ASSUMED (review): S4 prints the band as the PDF's own Chromium header and footer (Gotenberg header.html/footer.html, lifted from template data-pdf in the page), so it sits in the 0.4 in margins on every sheet and never covers content; whether a company is a demo is the Tenancy port DemoCompanies answered by Demo. Alternatives: a CSS position:fixed band (repeats per sheet but overlaps the content's first and last lines), a band inside the document flow (first page only).
- [2026-10-18 03:34] ASSUMED (review): S6 removes a sandbox through the worker's five-minute tend, not at the moment of sign-out: a sandbox is done with once no live session of its owner was seen within the 2 h limit plus the 5-minute precision of the session record (ManageSessions::TOUCH_EVERY), and its visitor signed out or the claim is older than the limit. Alternatives: remove synchronously at sign-out (a long delete inside the sign-out request), a per-sandbox delayed message (a second clock beside the session's).
- [2026-10-18 03:34] ASSUMED (review): S7 leaves every sandbox (whatever its state) and its owner out of the operators' company and account lists, totals included, by passing the DemoCompanies/DemoAccounts ports' all() as the search's leftOut; DataHealth drops what it finds in a sandbox entirely (no operator notification). A detail page reached by id still answers. Alternatives: a demo column on company/user (Tenancy and Identity would carry Demo's concept), a filter the operator toggles (the ruling says hidden).
- [2026-10-18 03:50] ASSUMED (review): Demo sandbox builds travel on their own Messenger transport (demo), read by a demo-worker container of their own in every compose file; the worker keeps async and the schedule. Measured: a reset mail asked during a build arrived after 13.7 s with one consumer, 0.9 s with two; CI's e2e red on a05e0b43 was two invitation mails behind the tend's first pool build. The demo-worker leaves the release's row steps to the api (TWES_RELEASE_STEPS=api) rather than run converge beside the worker. Alternatives: one worker consuming async before demo (a running build still holds a mail 12-30 s), splitting a build into step messages (the dataset is written under one moved clock).
- [2026-10-18 04:30] ASSUMED (review): Demo S8: the platform page gains a « Démo » card: demo.enabled is a switch saved without a version, like signup's (the operator's last word), and demo.sandboxes_max a count saved with its version (3 to 100), taken out of « Délais », which showed it as « De 3 à 100 jours »; the card's intro names no duration, since app.demo.idle_ttl is a parameter. Basis: the S8 scope in this plan and the signup switches' pattern. Alternatives: the count left among the delays with a per-key unit (a unit field the API does not declare).
- [2026-10-18 04:30] ASSUMED (review): The demo dataset's direct invoices sell services and untracked goods only; lot- and serial-tracked articles leave through the delivery notes, which name their lot or serial number. Basis: DemoFixturesTest's new invariant (every invoice issued without a delivery note took its goods out of stock), red on FAC-2026-08-00021 selling 4 PC-PORT whose 5 serials the notes had taken. Alternatives: name serials on the invoice lines (the dataset would track which notes took which piece).
- [2026-10-18 04:30] ASSUMED (review): setting-labels.sh now also requires a label for every whole-number platform setting, which the operators' page draws by its declared label: settings.platform.licensing_hold_days showed as a raw key under « Délais » and is labelled. Text, bool and enum platform settings stay out (their cards carry their own texts). Basis: the gate's own premise (no page reads a platform label) was false for these. Alternatives: label every platform setting (texts no screen reads, inside a budget 1 kB from full).
- [2026-10-18 05:42] ASSUMED (review): #39 (d) delivery notes reach invoices only through their own ports: InvoicedLines (taken, firstCarrying) and InvoiceDrafts (createFromLines returns the draft's id, appendLines, ofId as a side-effect-free read) with DN-owned values InvoiceLineOfNote, InvoiceHeaderOfNotes, InvoiceToAddTo, CarryingInvoice; InvoiceAnswer takes the invoice id. ModuleBoundariesTest now holds Domain classes to the contract test and never counts another module's repository as a contract; the 131 existing crossings (44 module -> class pairs) wait in AWAITING_A_RULING, keyed by module and class rather than file, none of them delivery notes -> invoices. Because the red run showed InvoiceHeader and InvoiceLineDetails are not ctor-only values, so they could not stay as contracts. Side effect: a note line that no longer makes an invoice line is now refused inside the port call, after addToDraft's customer check, so such a note of another customer answers 422 on invoiceId rather than on the line. Alternatives: file-keyed list (262 lines, a new file naming an already-waiting class would red), or keeping the invoice values (refused by the test).
- [2026-10-18 07:31] ASSUMED (review): Row 259: a quote with a discount on the whole is refused on an existing draft (409 on discountAmount), and « Facturer » then offers no picker and makes a new invoice — because spreading it pro rata over the draft's other lines would move tax between rates. Alternatives: allocate the discount per line; carry it as a negative line.
- [2026-10-18 07:31] ASSUMED (review): Row 259: an invoice's quoteId stays the quote it was drafted from; a quote added later is recorded on the quote (invoiced, with the invoice id) and in its audit entry, not on the invoice — because the invoice holds one quote link today. Follow-up: the invoice page lists every quote it carries, as it lists its delivery notes. Alternatives: a quote-source link per line now.
- [2026-10-18 07:31] ASSUMED (review): Row 259: deposit invoices and credit notes are refused as targets for both a quote's and a delivery note's lines, in the domain (Invoice::addLines) and at both callers; an added deposit give-back line is checked as on create (same customer, never on a deposit) — because the same hole was open for delivery notes, and goods added to a deposit would be charged twice. Alternatives: refuse at the callers only.
- [2026-10-18 08:01] ASSUMED (review): Row 259 wording: the picker asks « Sur quelle facture ce devis est-il facturé ? » / « Where is this quote invoiced? » with the delivery notes' help text, and a quote added to a draft toasts « Le devis a été ajouté à la facture en brouillon. » / « The quote was added to the draft invoice. » (a new invoice keeps « Le devis est devenu une facture en brouillon. ») — because they mirror the delivery notes' question and say what happened. Alternatives: the developer's own words.
- [2026-10-18 08:30] ASSUMED (review): Step 3 S0: the five stored language columns (user.locale, company.locale, signup.locale, legal_text.language, invoice.language) widen to VARCHAR(35) now, the length RFC 5646 asks a store to keep — because it is free before the first release tag and a two-release column swap after it, though VARCHAR(5) already fits every variant ruled (fr-TN, en-GB, ar-TN). Alternatives: keep 5 until a script subtag is needed. The inventory agent ran on the session's model (autonomous mode, no model question).
- [2026-10-18 09:27] ASSUMED (review): Step 3 S1: the language catalogue is two committed copies (api/config/languages.json, web/src/app/shared/i18n/languages.json) held equal by CatalogueLanguagesTest, run in CI's api job, rather than a separate shell gate — because the web image cannot read api/ and one PHPUnit assertion does the gate's work. Alternatives: a shared file copied at build; a scripts/gates script.
- [2026-10-18 09:27] ASSUMED (review): Step 3 S1: the OpenAPI contract no longer enumerates a legal text's or an invoice's language (plain string, a BCP 47 tag) — because a language added to the catalogue would otherwise need a new client. Alternatives: keep the enum and regenerate on each language.
- [2026-10-18 09:27] ASSUMED (review): Step 3 S1: the document-language select keeps naming each language in the screen's language (« Anglais »), through settings.choices labels the setting-labels gate now reads from the catalogue; the interface language menu keeps naming each in itself — because a seller picks a customer's language in their own words. Alternatives: autonyms everywhere; Intl.DisplayNames (lower-case in French).
- [2026-10-18 09:27] ASSUMED (review): Step 3 S1: LegalLanguage becomes a value object over a BCP 47 tag; a page missing in a language is read in the interface's languages, the default first (today fr then en, as before); the importer reads a customer's language by tag or by its ICU name in any screen language, ignoring case and accents — because the enum and the hand list were the code a language would otherwise need. Alternatives: keep the enum and add a case per language.
- [2026-10-18 09:53] ASSUMED (review): S3: a person's own screen language (presentation.language at user level) becomes their account's language through a PersonalSettingChanged event that ChangeSettings publishes after commit; going back to the company's choice leaves the account on the last language chosen, and a language the screens do not speak is never an account's. A one-off UPDATE migration gives accounts the choice made before. Alternatives: read the setting at mail time (worker has no request context, and every mail writer would need the settings engine); clear the account to the default on reset (loses the person's last explicit choice).
- [2026-10-18 09:53] ASSUMED (review): S3: a company's language at creation must be a catalogue language documents print in (DocumentLanguage constraint replaces Assert\Locale, so fr_TN, de and ar are refused); an invited person's new account takes the company's language only when the screens speak it, else the catalogue default; the account constructor has no built-in 'fr'. Alternative: keep Assert\Locale (accepts any ICU locale, which nothing downstream can print or speak).
- [2026-10-18 09:53] ASSUMED (review): S3 known issue, parked: an operator-created company whose language differs from the platform default still prints documents in the default until document.language is set at company level, because a setting's default cannot depend on the company. Signup is unaffected (both presets document in fr). Options for review: write the company-level document.language at creation, or let the parties chain fall back to the company's language. Not a release blocker for the first client (French).
- [2026-10-18 10:44] ASSUMED (review): A company opened without a language (POST /api/companies with no locale) takes the first language its country's fiscal preset prints in; the platform page sends none, so a country's language lives only in its preset — because the language gate found it repeated per country in the web, and the signup already resolves it from the preset. - [2026-10-18 23:55] AGREED: The tree freezes Tuesday 2026-10-20 at 20:00 Tunis time: after it only fixes of real defects land, and a named release-candidate commit, CI green, is handed over with what was and was not certified; refactors and harmonization resume after the install.
- [2026-10-18 23:56] AGREED: In the audit rounds a finding is one of four kinds: A a defect (the app does not do what is ruled or documented), B canon alignment (two screens differ where a written canon exists), C an improvement inside a ruling (unspecified, plainly serving a ruling, small and reversible), D a product change (a new or removed capability, a changed rule, a fiscal or legal impact, data a shop relies on, or choosing or changing a canon itself). A and B are fixed directly and logged; C only when advisor() agrees, logged ASSUMED (review); D is parked on one marking page with options and a pick, and work moves on.

## Formal Plan
No fixed date (2026-10-20 00:49, replacing the Wednesday 10:00 deadline): the release is tagged once every step below
is built and the three-lens panel on a frozen commit is clean. Order (2026-10-20 01:01): steps 12-16 (import, delete,
merge) first, then 17-22 and the open steps above, then audit and maturity rounds until no security, leak, fiscal or
legal issue is left. Each step is sliced test-first, gated (`make gate`), committed by pathspec
from the index; pushes at the batch size or a step's end, CI read job by job. Every step's completion note says what
was and was not certified by execution.

1. Release core. The shop's install: production images (`compose.prod.yaml`, `compose.backup.yaml`) on Windows 10/11
   (Docker Desktop and WSL 2), Linux and macOS, started with the machine; reached on localhost and over HTTPS on the LAN
   address with a local certificate authority the phones trust once. An update is one command: it takes an encrypted
   backup first, migrates, checks health and rolls back to the backup and the previous images when anything fails;
   migrations are additive (expand, then contract in a later release) so the previous version still runs on the new
   schema. A security pass over what an on-premises install exposes: secrets generated per install, no default
   password, the operator's first sign-in, ports, headers, the backup's encryption.
2. The demo sandbox: `demo@example.com` signs into a private copy of the demo company made for that sign-in; every
   document stamped « DÉMO — sans valeur fiscale », mail captured, nothing reaching the shop's company; deleted at
   sign-out or after 2 h idle, by a scheduled task that is idempotent.
3. Language foundations and RTL (row 338's foundations): a language is data (tag, name, direction, script, plurals,
   font); the person's language is one BCP 47 tag; the layout mirrors through logical properties; Arabic's font
   self-hosted; a gate refuses a language code written in code.
4. Proof pass of every built module in fr, en and ar: each workflow and its edge cases end to end, defects fixed at
   their root, the client's Excel file mapped into the importer as a saved mapping.
5. Reports.
6. Supplier orders and goods receipts (row 81).
7. The Arabic texts and printed documents, reviewed by the developer in the app and the source.
8. Guides, help and README on the final screens.
12. One reference declaration per subject: every record that names it, through a foreign key or not
    (`purchase_order.vendor_id`, `setting.level_id`, JSONB tax lists, lot codes), read by the delete, the bin, the
    merges and the import undo alike.
13. Delete while unused (classes of 00:36) and « Récemment supprimés »: each deleted record 24 hours, « Restaurer ».
14. Merges on the alias model (01:32): vendors, customers, products (stock by a transfer pair), product and expense
    categories, drafts; refused across matricules, regimes, units, kinds, tracking; « Annuler la fusion » 24 hours.
15. The « à vérifier » marker and its automatic reasons (01:40); several vendors per product; the categories import;
    the cost rule and its two settings (01:43).
16. Imports to 5000 rows, one undoable run per file, « Annuler cet import » and « Imports récents ».
17. The client's workbook converted locally into those files, never committed, once its open points are answered.
18. The « Installation » card on a shop's computer: the app's request read by twes tick, the night window, « Mettre à
    jour maintenant », release notes in fr, en, ar, missed windows told, the demo off, sandboxes 1 ready and 3 at most.
19. The « shop alive » heartbeat from twes tick to the monitor.
20. Defects found by the scope review in existing code: personal values in a database error's DETAIL reaching the
    monitor, `set_value` expanding escapes into the values file, release news told to demo sandboxes' owners.
21. The rest: Arabic fixes (round 6), round 5's F7, the accessibility round, guide 8b, the list floor sweep.
22. Audit and maturity rounds, then the one three-lens panel on a frozen commit, then the release's certification.

### Known issues
- Playwright's WebKit keeps an icon font a reload cut short as failed for the reloaded page: every icon then reads as
  its name. The e2e no longer reload a page still loading it (`signIn` waits, `e2e/icon-font.ts`); whether Safari on
  an iPhone does the same to a person reloading while the app opens is not known. Try it on a real iPhone (2026-10-23).
- `make mutants` once graded a web mutant INVALID (« no tally, exit 1 ») whose log held `Tests 1 failed`; its verdict
  pattern matched that log 300 times in 300 in the container, and two reruns killed it. Fail-safe (an INVALID never
  counts as a kill) but unexplained: the log may still have been written after the verdict read it. Rerun before
  rewriting such a mutant.
- `UnnamedFiles` reads the foreign keys to `file` from the catalogue once per owned table when a removal ends, then
  once more to decide which files are named (nine reads for a product): correct, a P2 to read them once per call.
- A product deleted with its vendor links, then that vendor deleted (nothing names it once the links went with the
  product): the product's restore refuses whole as `restore_conflict` until the vendor is restored first
  (`ProductRemovalTest`). The toast says that a record it names may have been deleted since, without naming which.
- Merges (A6+) cannot yet move only a draft's lines: `movingOnlyWhere` reads a column of the line's own table, and a
  line's status is its document's; the product references on invoice, quote, delivery note and order lines are
  declared `Stays` until merges are built.
- The activity journal says « l’article » in French where the screens and « Récemment supprimés » say « produit »:
  the journal's own words were kept for its new lines, the split is for the canon pass.
- `web/e2e/vendors.spec.ts`'s header still says vendors are never deleted; trim it when the file is next edited.
- On an Arabic screen a telephone number reads « +216 74 000 000 » in its field and in the contacts list (measured
  glyph by glyph, `direction.spec.ts`, 2026-10-19), as the printed documents do. The list holds it through its value's
  `dir="auto"` (removing it reds the scenario); the field through Chromium's own stylesheet for a tel input (computed
  `direction: ltr` with no attribute of ours), which is the browser's, so it is certified on Chromium (Edge) only.

- Audit round 1 (2026-10-19, the shop's first day on a fresh Tunisian company, walked in the browser): the first invoice
  went out at 0 % VAT, since a new product started with no tax although the preset marks TVA19 its default; the form now
  ticks the company's default line taxes on a new product, and an imported new product left without a tax is pointed out
  (`no_taxes`, D9 asks whether it should take the default instead). An order received and an invoice issued moved no
  stock: `article.stock_tracking` is off on a new company, and the trade that would switch it on for a shop (§ 8 row 94)
  is not built; the shop's installation ticks it (TODO.md), and the receive dialog still says nothing on a line whose
  product keeps no stock. The receipt's button read « Réceptionner # ligne », ICU's `#` being no part of `PluralCompiler`
  (the parity spec now refuses it); a saved order redrew its lines, losing the field typed into (NG0956). Left: a
  refusal at issue names the customer's record and the company profile as words, not links; a customer's tax number is
  labelled optional while its hint says a business needs it. Walked again on a new company with « Suivre le stock »
  ticked in Paramètres: the product form starts at TVA 19 %, a receipt of 20 and a sale of 3 leave 17, and the invoice
  prints TVA 19 % (7,125 on 37,500), the stamp duty and 45,625 TTC.
- Audit round 2 (one canon): an order's unit price read « 7,8000 » beside a total of « 156,000 », where every other
  screen and the printed order write a price at the currency's scale, finer only when the price itself is; the order now
  answers its `currencyScale` and the page writes its prices and totals at it. Left as it is: the order's line editor
  shows a price as typed (« 7,8 ») where the invoice's editor writes it at the scale (« 12,500 »), since a new order
  knows no scale before it is saved. The same action read differently from one document to the next: the invoice's
  « Aperçu PDF » (« Aperçu du PDF » elsewhere, « PDF preview » in English), the quote's Arabic « تحميل » for download
  (« تنزيل » everywhere else, « تحميل » being loading), the order's « Enregistrer » for « Enregistrer le brouillon », its
  « Envoyer la commande » although nothing sends it (now « Marquer envoyée », as the quote is « Marquer envoyé » for that
  reason), and the delivery note's « Confirmer l’annulation » where every other confirmation names its action. The parity
  spec now holds those actions alike on the four documents. Formats hold one canon: no screen formats a date or a
  number but through `FormatFacade` and its pipes. One exception found and fixed: a recurring invoice's or expense's
  schedule named its weekday and month in the bare language, so a Tunisian company's Arabic screen said « يناير » beside
  dates written in ar-TN, whose January is « جانفي »; the schedule now takes `FormatFacade.locale()`
  (`recurring-expenses-page.spec.ts`, red first, mutant killed).
